\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{2}(\nu x,x^{\ell})({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x(x^{!}).}x.[\,];x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}[\,];{\bf 0}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u};x^{\ell}({x}_{1}).

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{u,x_{1}};y_{2}[\,];{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}x_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};x^{\ell}(y_{1}).

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(x_{1}).(x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{2}).

\displaystyle(y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{y_{2}}[\,]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overline{x}(x^{!}).}(!x^{!}(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\overline{x}[\,]))))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x,x^{\ell},x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x[\,]};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}[\,];{\bf 0}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u};x^{\ell}({x}_{1}).x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.

\displaystyle\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{u,x_{1}};y_{2}[\,];{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}x_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};x^{\ell}(y_{1}).x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(x_{1}).(x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{y_{2}}[\,]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overline{x}[\,]})))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{1}).}(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.[\,];{\bf 0}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u};x^{\ell}({x}_{1}).

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{u,x_{1}};y_{2}.[\,];{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}x_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};x^{\ell}(y_{1}).}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(x_{1}).(x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{y_{2}}[\,]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},y_{1},x^{!})(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}[\,];{\bf 0}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u};x^{\ell}({x}_{1}).x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{2}).

\displaystyle(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{u,x_{1}};y_{2}[\,];{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}x_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(x_{1}).(x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\displaystyle\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{y_{2}}[\,]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}(\nu x_{1},x^{!})({x_{1}}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};[{x_{1}}\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}(\nu x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\llfloor{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

Figure 11:
Illustrating operational correspondence, following Example
32
.

5
Concluding Remarks

Summary

We have extended the line of work we developed in
[
17
]
, on resource
\lambda
-calculi with firm logical foundations via typed concurrent processes. We presented
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, a resource calculus with
non-determinism and explicit failures, with dedicated treatment for linear and unrestricted resources. By means of examples, we illustrated the expressivity, (lazy) semantics, and design decisions underpinning
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, and introduced a class of well-formed expressions based on intersection types, which includes fail-prone expressions. To bear witness to the logical foundations of
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, we defined and proved correct a typed encoding into the concurrent calculus
\mathsf{s}\pi
, which subsumes the one in
[
17
]
.
We plan to study key properties for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
(such as solvability and normalisation) by leveraging our typed encoding into
\mathsf{s}\pi
.

Related Work

With respect to previous resource calculi, a distinctive feature of

\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
is its support of explicit failures, which may arise depending on the interplay between (i) linear and unrestricted occurrences of variables in a term and (ii) associated resources in the bag. This feature allows
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
to express variants of usual
\lambda
-terms (
\mathbf{I}
,
\Delta
,
\Omega
) not expressible in other resource calculi.

Related to
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
is Boudol’s work on a
\lambda
-calculus in which multiplicities can be infinite
[
1
,
3
]
.
An intersection type system is used to prove
adequacy
with respect to a testing semantics. However, failing behaviours as well as typability are not explored. Multiplicities can be expressed in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
: a linear resource is available
m
times when the linear bag contains
m
copies of it; the term fails if the corresponding number of linear variables is different from
m
.

Also related is the resource
\lambda
-calculus by Pagani and Ronchi della Rocca
[
16
]
, which includes linear and reusable resources; the latter are available in multisets, also called bags. In their setting,
M[N^{!}]
denotes an application of a term
M
to a resource
N
that can be used
ad libitum
. Standard terms such as
I
,
\Delta
and
\Omega
are expressed as
\lambda x.x
,
\Delta:=\lambda x.x[x^{!}]
, and
\Omega:=\Delta[\Delta^{!}]
, respectively; different variants are possible but cannot express the desired behaviour. A lazy reduction semantics is based on
baby
and
giant
steps: whereas the first consume one resource at each time, the second comprises several baby steps; combinations of the use of resources (by permuting resources in bags) are considered. A (non-idempotent) intersection type system is proposed: normalisation and a characterisation of solvability are investigated. Unlike our work, encodings into the
\pi
-calculus are not explored in
[
16
]
.

Appendix A
Appendix to Section
2

A.1
Diamond Property for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}

Proposition 33

(Diamond Property for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

For all
\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
\mathbb{N}_{2}
in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
s.t.
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
with
\mathbb{N}_{1}\neq\mathbb{N}_{2}
then
\exists\mathbb{M}
s.t.
\mathbb{N}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
,
\mathbb{N}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
.

Proof.

We give a short argument to convince the reader of this. Notice that an expression can only perform a choice of reduction steps when it is a nondeterministic sum of terms in which multiple terms can perform independent reductions. For simplicity sake we will only consider an expression
\mathbb{N}
that consist of two terms where
\mathbb{N}=N+M
. We also have that
N\longrightarrow N^{\prime}
and
M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}
. Then we let
\mathbb{N}_{1}=N^{\prime}+M
and
\mathbb{N}_{2}=N+M^{\prime}
by the
\mathtt{[R:ECont]}
rules. Finally we prove that
\mathbb{M}
exists by letting
\mathbb{M}=N^{\prime}+M^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix B
Appendix to Section
3

Here we prove subject reduction (SR) for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
(Theorem
17
). It follows from two substitution lemmas: one for substituting a linear variable (Lemma
34
) and another for an unrestricted variable (Lemma
35
). Proofs of both lemmas are standard, by structural induction; we give a complete proof of SR in Theorem
36
.

Lemma 34

(Linear Substitution Lemma for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

If
\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma\models M:\tau
,
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
, and
\Theta;\Delta\models N:\sigma

then

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
. There are three cases to be analyzed:

1.

M={x}
.

In this case,
\Theta;{x}:\sigma\models{x}:\sigma
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
{x}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}=N
, since
\Theta;\Delta\models N:\sigma
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

2.

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
, and one has the following derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{x}:\sigma\models M:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},{x}:\sigma\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different.

By IH, we get
\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
, which gives the following derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}})B:\tau

Therefore, from Def.
7
, one has
\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}})B:\tau
, and the result follows.

3.

M=M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
, with
{x}\not=y
, and one has the following derivation:

\Theta,y^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k},x:\sigma\models M:\tau\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,k
are positive integers.
By IH, we get
\Theta,y^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau
and

\Theta,y^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

From Def.
7
,
M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
, therefore,
\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models(M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle){\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau
and the result follows.
∎

Lemma 35

(Unrestricted Substitution Lemma for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

If
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M:\tau
,
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
,
\eta_{i}=\sigma
, and
\Theta;\cdot\models N:\sigma

then

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
. There are three cases to be analyzed:

1.

M={x}[i]
.

In this case,

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;{x}:\eta_{i}\models{x}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\cdot\models{x}[i]:\sigma

and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
{x}[i]{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=N
, since
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

2.

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
, and one has the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\models M:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau\quad\Theta,x^{!}:\sigma;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\varpropto\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different.

By IH, we get
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau
, which gives the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\varpropto\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}})B:\tau

From Def.
7
, one has
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}})B:\tau
, and the result follows.

3.

M=M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
, with
x\not=y
, and one has the following derivation:

\Theta,y^{!}:\epsilon,x:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k}\models M:\tau\quad\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{j},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\varpropto\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,k
are positive integers.
By IH, we get
\Theta,y^{!}:\epsilon,x:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau
and

\Theta,y^{!}:\epsilon,x:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k},\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau\quad\Theta,x:\sigma;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{j},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\varpropto\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Then,
M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
, and the result follows.

∎

Theorem 36

(SR in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
and
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
then
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau
.

Proof.

By structural induction on the reduction rules. We proceed by analysing the rule applied in
\mathbb{M}
. There are seven cases:

1.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=(\lambda x.M)B\longrightarrow M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
.
Since
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
, one has the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{{x}}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}abs]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models\lambda x.M:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(\lambda x.M)B:\tau

for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. Notice that

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{{x}}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau\quad\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Therefore,
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau
and the result follows.

2.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{\ell}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, where
C={\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\cdot\dots\cdot{\lbag N_{k}\rbag}
,
k\geq 1
,
\#({x},M)=k
and
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
. The reduction is as:

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}

C={\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\cdot\dots\cdot{\lbag N_{k}\rbag}\ ,\ k\geq 1

\#({x},M)=k

\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{\ell}]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N_{1}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{1})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+\cdots+M{\{\!|}N_{k}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{k})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

To simplify the proof we take
k=2
, as the case
k>2
is similar. Therefore,
c={\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\cdot{\lbag N_{2}\rbag}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{2}\models M:\tau

\Theta;\cdot\models U:\epsilon

\Pi

\Theta;\Delta\models{\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\cdot{\lbag N_{2}\rbag}:\sigma^{2}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{2},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

with
\Pi
the derivation

\Theta;\Delta_{1}\models N_{1}:\sigma

\Theta;\Delta_{2}\models N_{2}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}\mathtt{1}^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\text{-}\models{\mathtt{1}}:\omega

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Delta_{2}\models{\lbag N_{2}\rbag}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Delta\models{\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\cdot{\lbag N_{2}\rbag}:\sigma^{2}

where
\Delta=\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. By Lemma
34
, there exist derivations
\Pi_{1}
of
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma,\Delta_{1}\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau
and
\Pi_{2}
of
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma,\Delta_{2}\models M{\{\!|}N_{2}/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau
. Therefore, one has the following derivation where we omit the second case of the sum:

\Pi_{1}

\Theta;\cdot\models U:\epsilon

\Theta;\Delta\models{\lbag N_{2}\rbag}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Delta\models\lbag N_{2}\rbag\star U:(\sigma,\epsilon)

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta_{1}\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\lbag N_{2}\rbag}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau\

\vdots

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\lbag N_{2}\rbag}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Assuming
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\lbag N_{2}\rbag}\star B^{!}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\star B^{!}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, the result follows.

3.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{!}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, where
U=\lbag N_{1}\rbag^{!}\diamond\cdots\diamond\lbag N_{l}\rbag^{!}
and
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
. The reduction is as:

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\lbag N_{i}\rbag^{!}

\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{!}]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N_{i}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

By hypothesis, one has the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau

\Pi

\Theta;\cdot\models U:\epsilon

\Theta;\Delta\models{C}:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Where
\Pi
has the form

\Theta;\cdot\models N_{1}:\epsilon_{1}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\cdot\models\lbag N_{1}\rbag^{!}:\epsilon_{1}

\cdots

\Theta;\cdot\models N_{l}:\epsilon_{l}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\cdot\models\lbag N_{l}\rbag^{!}:\epsilon_{l}

\mathtt{[F{:}\diamond bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\cdot\models\lbag N_{1}\rbag^{!}\diamond\cdots\diamond\lbag N_{l}\rbag^{!}:\epsilon

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. Notice that if
\epsilon_{i}=\delta
and
\eta\varpropto\epsilon
then
\eta_{i}=\delta
By Lemma
35
, there exists a derivation
\Pi_{1}
of
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M{\{\!|}N_{i}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau
. Therefore, one has the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau

\Theta;\cdot\models U:\epsilon

\Theta;\Delta\models{C}:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N_{i}/{x[i]}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

4.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Fail^{\ell}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
\#({x},M)\neq\mathsf{size}(C)
and we can perform the reduction:

\#({x},M)\neq\mathsf{size}(C)

\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{mlfv}(M)\setminus x)\uplus\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\mathtt{[R:Fail^{\ell}]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow{}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

with
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
. By hypothesis, one has the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{2}\models M:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{2},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

From
\#(x,M)\neq\mathsf{size}(B)
we have that
j\neq k
. Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and we type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

5.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Fail^{!}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
,
U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}
and we can perform the reduction:

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}

\mathtt{[R:Fail^{!}]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

with
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. By hypothesis, one has the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. By Lemma
35
, there is a derivation
\Pi_{1}
of
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x[i]}{|\!\}}:\tau
. Therefore, one has the derivation: (the last rule applied is
\mathtt{[R:ex\text{-}sub]}
)

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau

\Theta;\cdot\models U:\epsilon

\Theta;\Delta\models{B}:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

6.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B
where
B=C\star U
,
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\dots\cdot\lbag N_{k}\rbag
,
k\geq 0
and we can perform the following reduction:

\mathsf{size}(C)=k

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}
. By hypothesis, one has

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

7.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
B=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\dots\cdot\lbag N_{k}\rbag
,
k\geq 1
and
one has the reduction:

\#(z,\widetilde{x})=\mathsf{size}(C)\quad\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$z$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{x}\setminus z)\uplus\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}
. By hypothesis, there exists a derivation:

\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j})=\widetilde{z}

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\hat{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

8.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:TCont]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=C[M]
and the reduction is as follows:

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{l}

\mathtt{[R:TCont]}

C[M]\longrightarrow C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{l}]

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{l}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
C
:

(a)

C=[\cdot]\ B
.

In this case
\mathbb{M}=M\ B
, for some
B
, and the following derivation holds:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
.
Since
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
and
M\longrightarrow M_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+M_{l}^{\prime}
, it follows by IH that
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+M_{l}^{\prime}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
. By applying
\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}
, one has
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{i}^{\prime}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
, for
i=1,\ldots,l
. Therefore, we may type the following:

\forall i\in{1,\ldots,l}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{i}^{\prime}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M_{i}^{\prime}\ B:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}_{1}\ B)+\cdots+(M^{\prime}_{l}\ B):\tau

Thus,
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M^{\prime}}:\tau
, and the result follows.

(b)

C=([\cdot])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

This case is similar to the previous.

9.

Rule
\mathtt{[R:ECont]}.

Then
\mathbb{M}=D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}]
where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}
then we can perform the following reduction:

\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\mathtt{[R:ECont]}

D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}]\longrightarrow D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

Hence
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
D
(
D=[\cdot]+\mathbb{N}
or
D=\mathbb{N}+[\cdot]
), and follows easily by induction hypothesis.

∎

B.1
Examples

This section contains examples illustrating the constructions and results given in Section
3
.

Example 37
.

The following is a wfderivation
\Pi_{2}
for the bag concatenation
\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!}:

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta^{\prime};x:\sigma\models x:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}\mathtt{1}^{\ell}]}

\Theta^{\prime};\text{-}\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{\ell}]}

\Theta^{\prime};x:\sigma\models\lbag x\rbag\cdot\mathtt{1}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}\mathtt{1}^{!}]}

\Theta^{\prime};\text{-}\models\mathtt{1}^{!}:\sigma^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Theta^{\prime};x:\sigma\models(\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!}):(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})

Example 38

(Cont.
37
)
.

The following is a well-formedness derivation (labels of the rules being applied are omitted) for term
\Delta_{4}=\lambda x.x[1](\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!})
:

\Theta,x^{!}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau;{x}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau\models{x}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau;\text{-}\models{x}[1]:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Pi_{2}

\eta\varpropto\sigma^{\prime}

\Theta,x^{!}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau;x:\sigma\models x[1](\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!}):\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\models\lambda x.(x[1](\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!})):(\sigma,(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau)\rightarrow\tau

Example 39
.

Below we show the wf-derivation for the bag

A=(\lbag x[1]\rbag\cdot\lbag x\rbag)\star\lbag x[2]\rbag^{!}
.

First, let
\Pi
be the following derivation:

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;{x}:\sigma_{3}\models{x}:\sigma_{3}

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\text{-}\models{x}[1]:\sigma_{3}

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models x:\sigma_{3}

\mathtt{[F{:}\mathtt{1}^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\text{-}\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models\lbag x\rbag\cdot\mathtt{1}:\sigma_{3}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models\lbag x[1]\rbag\cdot\lbag x\rbag:\sigma_{3}^{2}

From
\Pi
we can obtain the well-formedness derivation
\Pi_{A}
for
A
:

\Pi

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models\lbag x[1]\rbag\cdot\lbag x\rbag:\sigma_{3}^{2}

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{2}\models x:\sigma_{2}

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{!}]}

\mathtt{[F{:}bag^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models\underbrace{(\lbag x[1]\rbag\cdot\lbag x\rbag)\star\lbag x[2]\rbag^{!}}_{A}:(\sigma_{3}^{2},\sigma_{2})

where
\eta=\sigma_{3}\diamond\sigma_{2}
.

Example 40
.

of the bag

:

Example 41
.

To illustrate our well-formed rules, let
M
be the following
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
-term:

M=\lambda x.(y(\underbrace{(\lbag x[1]\rbag\cdot\lbag x\rbag)\star\lbag x[2]\rbag^{!})}_{A}\underbrace{(\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!})}_{B}).

To ease the notation
M
is an abstraction
\lambda x.((yA)\ B)
, where
A=(\lbag x[1]\rbag\cdot\lbag x\rbag)\star\lbag x[2]\rbag^{!}
and
B=\lbag x\rbag\star\mathtt{1}^{!}
.
From the derivation
\Pi_{A}
(Example
39
) we obtain the wf-derivation
\Pi_{A}^{\prime}
for the application
yA
:

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Delta\models y:(\sigma_{3}^{k},\eta^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow((\sigma_{3}^{j},\eta^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau)

\Pi_{A}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models A:(\sigma_{3}^{2},\sigma_{2})

\eta^{\prime\prime}\varpropto\sigma_{2}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3},\Delta\models yA:(\sigma_{3}^{j},\eta^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau

where
\eta=\sigma_{3}\star\sigma_{2}
, for some list type
\eta^{\prime}
and integers
k,j
. From the premise
\eta^{\prime\prime}\varpropto\sigma_{2}
it follows that
\eta^{\prime\prime}=\sigma_{2}\diamond\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}
for an arbitrary
\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}
. From the derivation
\Pi_{B}
(Example
40
) we obtain the well-formed derivation for term
M
:

\Pi_{A}^{\prime}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3},\Delta\models y\ A:(\sigma^{j}_{3},\eta^{\prime})\to\tau

\Pi_{B}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3}\models B:(\sigma_{3},\sigma^{\prime})

\eta^{\prime}\varpropto\sigma^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F:app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;x:\sigma_{3},x:\sigma_{3},\Delta\models(yA)B:\tau\qquad x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Delta)

\mathtt{[F{:}abs]}

\Theta;\Delta\models\lambda x.((yA)B):(\sigma_{3}^{2},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

where
\Delta=y:(\sigma_{3}^{k},\eta^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow((\sigma_{3}^{j},\eta^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau)
. From the premise
\eta^{\prime}\varpropto\sigma^{\prime}
we obtain that
\eta^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}\diamond\eta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
, where
\sigma^{\prime}
is an arbitrary strict type and
\eta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
is an arbitrary list type.

Appendix C
Appendix to Subsection
4.1

Definition 42

(Structural Congruence)
.

Structural congruence
is defined as the least congruence relation on processes such that:

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{1.5cm}}c@{\hspace{1.5cm}}c}P\equiv_{\alpha}Q\Rightarrow P\equiv Q\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\bf 0}\equiv P\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q\equiv Q\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P\\
(\nu x){\bf 0}\equiv{\bf 0}\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&(P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q)\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}R\equiv P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(Q\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}R)\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&[x\leftrightarrow y]\equiv[y\leftrightarrow x]\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{1.5cm}}c}x\not\in\mathit{fn}(P)\Rightarrow((\nu x)P)\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q\equiv(\nu x)(P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q)\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&(\nu x)(\nu y)P\equiv(\nu y)(\nu x)P\\
P\oplus(Q\oplus R)\equiv(P\oplus Q)\oplus R\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&P\oplus Q\equiv Q\oplus P\\
(\nu x)(P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(Q\oplus R))\equiv(\nu x)(P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q)\oplus(\nu x)(P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}R)\hfil\hskip 42.67912pt&{\bf 0}\oplus{\bf 0}\equiv{\bf 0}\end{array}\end{array}

Appendix D
Appendix to Subsection
4.2

We need a few auxiliary notions to formalize reduction for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
.

Definition 43

(Head)
.

We amend Definition
6
for the case of terms in
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{3cm}}l}\mathsf{head}({x})={x}\hfil\hskip 85.35826pt&\mathsf{head}({x}[i])={x}[i]\\
\mathsf{head}(M\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M)\hfil\hskip 85.35826pt&\mathsf{head}(\lambda x.(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]))=\lambda x.(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\\
\mathsf{head}(M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M)\hfil\hskip 85.35826pt&\mathsf{head}(M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M)\end{array}\\
\mathsf{head}((M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\hskip 28.45274pt\mathsf{head}(\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}})=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\\
\mathsf{head}(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])=\begin{cases}{x}&\text{If $\mathsf{head}(M)=y\text{ and }y\in{\widetilde{x}}$}\\
\mathsf{head}(M)&\text{Otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\end{array}

Definition 44

(Linear Head Substitution)
.

Given an
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)=x
, the linear substitution of a term
N
for the head variable
x
of the term
M
, written
M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}
is inductively defined as:

\displaystyle x{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

\displaystyle=N

\displaystyle(M\ B){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ B

\displaystyle(M\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

\displaystyle=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ \llfloor U/{y}\rrceil

\displaystyle x\not=y

\displaystyle(M{\langle\!|}L/{y}{|\!\rangle}){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

\displaystyle=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ {\langle\!|}L/{y}{|\!\rangle}

\displaystyle x\not=y

\displaystyle((M[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small${{y}}$}\rangle\!\rangle){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

\displaystyle=(M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow{y}]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle x\not=y

\displaystyle(M[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

\displaystyle=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]

\displaystyle x\not=y

Following Def.
8
, we define contexts for terms and expressions.
While expression contexts are as in Def.
8
; the term contexts for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
involve explicit linear and unrestricted substitutions, rather than an explicit substitution: this is due to the reduction strategy we have chosen to adopt, as we always wish to evaluate explicit substitutions first.
We assume that the terms that fill in the holes respect the conditions on explicit linear substitutions (i.e., variables appear in a term only once, shared variables must occur in the context), similarly for explicit unrestricted substitutions.

Definition 45

(Evaluation Contexts)
.

Contexts for terms and expressions are defined by the following grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rl}C[\cdot],C^{\prime}[\cdot]&::=([\cdot])B\mid([\cdot]){\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}\mid([\cdot])\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\mid([\cdot])[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\mid([\cdot])[\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\\
D[\cdot],D^{\prime}[\cdot]&::=M+[\cdot]\mid[\cdot]+M\end{array}

The result of replacing a hole with a
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
-term
M
in a context
C[\cdot]
, denoted with
C[M]
, has to be a term in
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
.

This way, e.g., the hole in context
C[\cdot]=([\cdot]){\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
cannot be filled with
y
, since
C[y]=(y){\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
is not a well-defined term.
Indeed,
M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
requires that
x
occurs exactly once within
M
.
Similarly, we cannot fill the hole with
\mathtt{fail}^{z}
with
z\neq x
, since
C[\mathtt{fail}^{z}]=(\mathtt{fail}^{z}){\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
is also not a well-defined term, for the same reason.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{lfv}({x})=\{{x}\}\\
\mathsf{lfv}({x}[i])=\emptyset\\
\mathsf{lfv}({\mathtt{1}})=\emptyset\\
\mathsf{lfv}({\lbag M\rbag})=\mathsf{lfv}(M)\\
\mathsf{lfv}(\lbag M\rbag^{!})=\mathsf{lfv}(M)\\
\mathsf{lfv}(C\star U)=\mathsf{lfv}(C)\\
\mathsf{lfv}({\lbag M\rbag}\cdot C)=\mathsf{lfv}({M})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{lfv}(M\ B)=\mathsf{lfv}(M)\cup\mathsf{lfv}(B)\\
\mathsf{lfv}(\lambda x.M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])=\mathsf{lfv}(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\!\setminus\!\{{x}\}\\
\mathsf{lfv}(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=(\mathsf{lfv}(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\setminus\{{x}\})\uplus\mathsf{lfv}(B)\\
\mathsf{lfv}(M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{lfv}({M})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(N)\\
\mathsf{lfv}(M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)=\mathsf{lfv}({M})\\
\mathsf{lfv}(\mathbb{M}+\mathbb{N})=\mathsf{lfv}(\mathbb{M})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(\mathbb{N})\\
\mathsf{lfv}(\mathtt{fail}^{{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{n}})=\{{x}_{1},\ldots,{x}_{n}\}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 12:
Free Variables for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
.

Operational Semantics

As in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, the reduction relation
\longrightarrow
on
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
operates lazily on expressions; it is defined by the rules in Fig.
13
, and relies on a notion of linear free variables given in Fig.
12
.

\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}

(\lambda x.(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]))B\longrightarrow(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}

M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\lbag N\rbag^{!}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}

M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

C=\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdots\lbag M_{k}\rbag

M\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

\!M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{x_{k}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

k\neq\mathsf{size}(C)

\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus\{\widetilde{x}\})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{\ell}]}

M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(M)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}

M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}

\mathsf{size}(C)=|{\widetilde{x}}|

\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}

(\mathtt{fail}^{{\widetilde{x}}\uplus\widetilde{y}}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}

\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{lfv}(N)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup x}{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{4}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

Figure 13:
Reduction Rules for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
(contextual rules omitted)

As expected, rule
\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}
results into an explicit substitution
M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, where
B=C\star U
is a bag with a linear part
C
and an unrestricted part
U
.

In the case
|\widetilde{x}|=k=\mathsf{size}(C)
and
M\neq\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
, this explicit substitution expands into a sum of terms involving explicit linear and unrestricted substitutions
{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
and
\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
, which are the ones to reduce into a head substitution, via rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}
. Intuitively, rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}
“distributes” an explicit substitution into a sum of terms involving explicit linear substitutions; it considers all possible permutations of the elements in the bag among all shared variables. Explicit linear/unrestricted substitutions evolve either into a head substitution
{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}
(with
N\in B
), via rule
\mathtt{[RS:Fetch^{\ell}]}
, or
{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
(with
U\in B
) via rule
\mathtt{[RS:Fetch^{!}]}
, depending on whether the head of the term is a linear or an unrestricted variable.

In the case
|\widetilde{x}|=k\neq\mathsf{size}(C)
or
M=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
, the term
M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
will be a redex of either rule
\mathtt{[RS:Fail^{\ell}]}
or
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{2}]}
. The latter has a side condition
|\widetilde{x}|=\mathsf{size}(C)
, because we want to give priority for application of
\mathtt{[RS:Fail^{\ell}]}
when there is a mismatch of linear variables and the number of linear resources.
Rule
\mathtt{[RS:Fail^{!}]}
applies to an unrestricted substitution
M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
when the head of
M
is an unrestricted variable, say
x[i]
, that aims to consume the
i
-th component of the bag
U
which is empty, i.e.,
U_{i}=1^{!}
; then the term reduces to a term where all the head of
M
is substituted by
\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}
, the explicit unrestricted substitution is not consumed and continues in the resulting term. Consuming rules
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{1}]},\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{3}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{4}]}
the term
\mathtt{fail}
consume either a bag, or an explicit linear substitution, or an explicit unrestricted substitution, respectively.

Notice that the left-hand sides of the reduction rules in
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
do not interfere with each other.
Similarly to
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, reduction in
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
satisfies a
diamond property
.

Example 46
.

We continue to illustrate the different behaviors of the terms below w.r.t. the reduction rules for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
(Fig.
13
):

1.

The case with a linear variable
x
in which the linear bag has size one, is close to the standard meaning of applying an identity function to a term:

\displaystyle(\lambda x.x_{1}[x_{1}\leftarrow x])\lbag N^{\prime}\rbag\star U^{\prime}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R:Beta]}}x_{1}[x_{1}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\lbag N^{\prime}\rbag\star U^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}}x_{1}{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{\ell}]}}x_{1}{\{\!|}N^{\prime}/x_{1}{|\!\}}\llfloor U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil=N^{\prime}\llfloor U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil

2.

The case of an abstraction of one unrestricted variable that aims to consume the first element of the unrestricted bag, which fails to contain a resource in the first component.

\displaystyle(\lambda x.x[1][\leftarrow x])\mathtt{1}\star\mathtt{1}^{!}\diamond U^{\prime}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R:Beta]}}x[1][\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}\star\mathtt{1}^{!}\diamond U^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}}x[1]\llfloor\mathtt{1}^{!}\diamond U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}}x[1]{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[1]{|\!\}}\llfloor\mathtt{1}^{!}\diamond U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil=\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}\llfloor\mathtt{1}^{!}\diamond U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil

3.

The case of an abstraction of one unrestricted variable that aims to consume the
i
th component of the unrestricted bag
U^{\prime}
. In the case
C^{\prime}=\mathtt{1}
and
U^{\prime}_{i}\neq 1^{!}
the reduction is:

\displaystyle(\lambda x.x[i][\leftarrow x])C^{\prime}\diamond U^{\prime}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R:Beta]}}x[i][\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C^{\prime}\diamond U^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}}x[i]\llfloor C^{\prime}\diamond U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}}x[i]{\{\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor C^{\prime}\diamond U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil=N\llfloor C^{\prime}\diamond U^{\prime}/{x}\rrceil

where
U^{\prime}_{i}=\lbag N^{\prime}\rbag^{!}
.
Otherwise,
U^{\prime}_{i}=1^{!}
and the reduction relies again on the size of the linear bag
C
: if
\#(x,x[i])=\mathsf{size}(C^{\prime})
the reduction ends with an application of
\mathtt{[R:\mathtt{fail}^{!}]}
; otherwise, it ends with an application
\mathtt{[R:\mathtt{fail}^{\ell}]}
.

D.1
Well-formedness rules for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}

Similarly to
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
we present a set “well-formedness” rules for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
-terms, -bags and -expressions, based on an intersection type system for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, defined upon strict, multiset, list, tuple types, as introduced for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and presented in Fig.
14
. Linear contexts
\Gamma,\Delta
and unrestricted contexts
\Theta,\Upsilon
are the same as in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, as well as well-formedness judgements
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M}:\sigma
.

Definition 47

(Well-formedness in
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

An expression
\mathbb{M}
is well formed if there exists a
\Theta,\Gamma
and a
\tau
such that
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
is entailed via the rules in Fig.
14
.

\mathtt{[FS{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta;{x}:\sigma\models{x}:\sigma

\Theta,{x}:\eta;{x}:\eta_{i},\Delta\models{x}:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}var^{!}]}

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Delta\models{x}[i]:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}\mathtt{1}^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\text{-}\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

\mathtt{[FS{:}\mathtt{1}^{!}]}

\Theta;\text{-}\models\mathtt{1}^{!}:\sigma

\Theta;\Gamma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS\!:\!weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\omega\models M[\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\sigma

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{N}:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}+\mathbb{N}:\sigma

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{k}\models M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau\quad{x}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)

\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\lambda x.(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]):(\sigma^{k},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma\models M:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M\ B:\tau

\Theta;\Gamma\models C:\sigma^{k}

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\eta

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\eta)

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\Theta;\text{-}\models V:\eta

\mathtt{[FS{:}\diamond-bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\text{-}\models U\diamond V:\epsilon\diamond\eta

\Theta;\text{-}\models M:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\text{-}\models\lbag M\rbag^{!}:\sigma

\Theta;\Gamma\models M:\sigma

\Theta;\Delta\models C:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models\lbag M\rbag\cdot C:\sigma^{k+1}

{x}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)\quad k\not=0

\Theta;\Gamma,{x}_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,{x}_{k}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{k}\models M[{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models N:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma\models M:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub]}

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)=\widetilde{x}

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:\tau

Figure 14:
Well-Formedness Rules for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}

Well-formed rules for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
are essentially the same as the ones for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
. Rules
\mathtt{[FS:abs\text{-}sh]}
and
\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub]}
are modified to take into account the sharing construct
[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
. Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}
is exclusive for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and requires, for each
i=1,\ldots,k
, the variable assignment
x_{i}:\sigma
, to derive the well-formedness of
M[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\leftarrow x]:\tau
(in addition to variable assignments in
\Theta
and
\Gamma
).

Lemma 48

(Linear Substitution Lemma for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

If
\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma\models M:\tau
,
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
, and
\Theta;\Delta\models N:\sigma

then

\Gamma,\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
.
There are six cases to be analyzed:

1.

M={x}

In this case,
\Theta;{x}:\sigma\models{x}:\sigma
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
{x}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}=N
, since
\Delta\models N:\sigma
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

2.

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
, and the derivation is the following:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{x}:\sigma\models M^{\prime}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},{x}:\sigma\models M^{\prime}B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
, and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different. Since
\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
, by IH, the result holds for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Gamma_{1},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

which gives the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}})B:\tau

From Def.
44
,
(M^{\prime}B){\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}=(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}})B
,
and the result follows.

3.

M=M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
, for
y\neq x
. Therefore,

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\cdots,{y}_{k}:\delta,{x}:\sigma\models M^{\prime}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k},{x}:\sigma\models M^{\prime}[{y}_{1},\cdots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}
.
By IH, the result follows for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\cdots,{y}_{k}:\delta,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau

and we have the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\cdots,{y}_{k}:\delta,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

From Def.
44

M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
, and the result follows.

4.

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
with
x\not=y
,

\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega,{x}:\sigma\models M[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

and
M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}[\leftarrow{y}]
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega,\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

5.

If
M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}
then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x\not=y
,

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\delta,x:\sigma\models M:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models M^{\prime\prime}:\delta

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

and
M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\delta,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models M^{\prime\prime}:\delta

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

6.

If
M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil
then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, and the proofs is similar to the case above.

∎

Lemma 49

(Unrestricted Substitution Lemma for
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

If
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M:\tau
,
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
,
\eta_{i}=\sigma
, and
\Theta;\cdot\models N:\sigma

then

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
. There are three cases to be analyzed:

1.

M={x}[i]
.

In this case,

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;{x}:\eta_{i}\models{x}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}var^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\cdot\models{x}[i]:\sigma

and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
{x}[i]{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=N
, since
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

2.

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
, and one has the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\models M:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau\quad\Theta,x^{!}:\sigma;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\varpropto\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different.

By IH, we get
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x[i]}{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau
, which gives the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{2}\models B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\varpropto\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}})B:\tau

From Def.
7
,
M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
,
and the result follows.

3.

M=M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
, for
y\neq x
. Therefore,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\cdots,{y}_{k}:\delta\models M^{\prime}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\models M^{\prime}[{y}_{1},\cdots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow y]:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}
.
By IH, the result follows for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\cdots,{y}_{k}:\delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau

and we have the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\cdots,{y}_{k}:\delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

From Def.
44

M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
, and the result follows.

4.

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
with
x\not=y
,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega\models M[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

and
M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}[\leftarrow{y}]
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

5.

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
with
x\not=y
,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,{y}:\delta\models M:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Delta\models M^{\prime\prime}:\delta

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

and
M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,{y}:\delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Delta\models M^{\prime\prime}:\delta

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/{y}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

6.

M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta,y^{!}:\epsilon;\Gamma\models M:\tau\quad\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil:\tau

and
M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta,y^{!}:\epsilon;\Gamma\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau\quad\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\text{-}\models U:\eta

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil:\tau

∎

Theorem 50

(SR in
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
and
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
then
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau
.

Proof.

By structural induction on the reduction rule from Fig.
13
applied in
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}
.

1.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=(\lambda x.M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B
and the reduction is:

\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}

(\lambda x.M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B\longrightarrow M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,{x}_{j}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models\lambda x.M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(\lambda x.M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B:\tau

for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.
Notice that:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,{x}_{j}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Therefore
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau
and the result follows.

2.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\dots\cdot\lbag N_{k}\rbag
. The reduction is:

C=\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdots\lbag M_{k}\rbag

M\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

\!M[{x}_{1},\!\cdots\!,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{x_{k}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

and
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{x_{k}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

To simplify the proof we take
k=2
, as the case
k>2
is similar. Therefore,

•

C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\lbag N_{2}\rbag
; and

•

\mathsf{PER}(C)=\{\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\lbag N_{2}\rbag,\lbag N_{2}\rbag\cdot\lbag N_{1}\rbag\}
.

Since
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get a derivation: (we omit the labels
\mathtt{[FS:ex\text{-}sub]}
and
\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}
)

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,{x}_{2}:\sigma\models M:\tau\quad{x}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)\quad k\not=0

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{2}\models M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. Consider the wf derivation for
\Pi_{1,2}
: (we omit the labels
\mathtt{[FS:ex\text{-}sub^{!}]}
and
\mathtt{[FS:ex\text{-}sub^{\ell}]}
)

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,{x}_{2}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\Theta;\Delta_{1}\models N_{1}:\sigma

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{2}:\sigma,\Delta_{1}\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\Theta;\Delta_{2}\models N_{2}:\sigma

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/{x_{2}}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/{x_{2}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau

Similarly, we can obtain a derivation
\Pi_{2,1}
of
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{2}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{1}/{x_{2}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau
. Finally, applying
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
:

\Pi_{1,2}

\Pi_{2,1}

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/{x_{2}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil+M{\langle\!|}N_{2}/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{1}/{x_{2}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau

and the result follows.

3.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
. The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}

M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}

and
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma\models M:\tau\quad\Theta;\Delta\models N:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. By Lemma
48
, we obtain the derivation
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}{|\!\}}:\tau
.

4.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
. The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\lbag N\rbag^{!}

M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

and
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau

By Lemma
49
, we obtain the derivation
\Theta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau
.

5.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=C[M]
and the reduction is as follows:

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{k}

\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}

C[M]\longrightarrow C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{k}]

with
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=C[M]\longrightarrow C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{k}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
C
.
There are four cases:

(a)

C=[\cdot]\ B
.

In this case
\mathbb{M}=M\ B
, for some
B
. Since
\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M}:\tau
one has a derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. From
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
and the reduction
M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{k}
, one has by IH that
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots,M_{k}^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
, which entails
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{i}^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
, for
i=1,\ldots,k
, via rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
. Finally, we may type the following:

\forall i\in{1,\cdots,l}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M^{\prime}_{i}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M^{\prime}_{i}\ B:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}_{1}\ B)+\cdots+(M^{\prime}_{l}\ B):\tau

Since
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=(C[M^{\prime}_{1}])+\cdots+(C[M^{\prime}_{l}])=M_{1}^{\prime}B+\ldots+M_{k}^{\prime}B
, the result follows.

(b)

Cases
C=[\cdot]{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
and
C=[\cdot][\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
are similar to the previous.

(c)

Other cases proceed similarly.

6.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}ECont]}
.

This case is analogous to the previous.

7.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{\ell}]}.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\dots\cdot\lbag N_{l}\rbag
and the reduction is:

k\neq\mathsf{size}(C)

\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus\{\widetilde{x}\})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{\ell}]}

M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
. Since
\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\models\mathbb{M}
, one has a derivation:

\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k}\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models C\star U:(\sigma^{j},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}]\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. We may type the following:

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

since
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
contain assignments on the free variables in
M
and
B
.
Therefore,
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau
, by applying
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
, it follows that
\Theta;\Gamma\models\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau
,as required.

8.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}
.

Then
M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
and
B=U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}
and the reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(M)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}

M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

with
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
. By hypothesis, one has the derivation:

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma\models M:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau

By Lemma
49
, there exists a derivation
\Pi_{1}
of
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau
. Thus,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\models U:\epsilon

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil:\tau

9.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B
where
B=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\dots\cdot\lbag N_{k}\rbag
and the reduction is:

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U\longrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}

and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}
.
Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
, one has the derivation:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\models B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\varpropto\epsilon

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Theta;\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

The proof for the cases of
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}
,
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{4}]}
proceed similarly

∎

Appendix E
Appendix to Subsection
4.3

E.1
Encodability Criteria

We follow the criteria in
[
9
]
, a widely studied abstract framework for establishing the
quality
of encodings.
A
language

\mathcal{L}
is a pair: a set of terms and a reduction semantics
\longrightarrow
