on terms (with reflexive, transitive closure denoted
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\longrightarrow}}
).
A correct encoding translates terms of a source language
\mathcal{L}_{1}=(\mathcal{M},\longrightarrow_{1})
into terms of a target language
\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{P},\longrightarrow_{2})
by respecting certain criteria.
The criteria in
[
9
]
concern
untyped
languages; because we treat
typed
languages, we follow
[
14
]
in requiring that encodings satisfy the following criteria:

1.

Type preservation:
For every well-typed
M
, it holds that
\llbracket M\rrbracket
is well-typed.

2.

Operational Completeness:
For every
{M},{M}^{\prime}
such that
{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{1}{M}^{\prime}
, it holds that
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{2}\approx_{2}\llbracket{M}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.

3.

Operational Soundness:
For every
M
and
P
such that
\llbracket M\rrbracket\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{2}P
, there exists an
M^{\prime}
such that
M\longrightarrow^{*}_{1}M^{\prime}
and
P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{2}\approx_{2}\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket
.

4.

Success Sensitiveness:
For every
{M}
, it holds that
M\checkmark_{1}
if and only if
\llbracket M\rrbracket\checkmark_{2}
, where
\checkmark_{1}
and
\checkmark_{2}
denote a success predicate in
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{P}
, respectively.

In addition to these semantic criteria, we shall also consider
compositionality
: a composite source term is encoded as the combination of the encodings of its sub-terms.
Success sensitiveness complements completeness and soundness, giving information about observable behaviors.
The so-called success predicates
\checkmark_{1}
and
\checkmark_{2}
serve as a minimal notion of
observables
; the criterion then says that observability of success of a source term implies observability of success in the corresponding target term, and vice-versa.

E.2
Correctness of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

The correctness of the encoding from
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
from
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
to
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
relies on an encoding on contexts (Def.
51
), auxiliary propositions (Propositions
52
and
53
) for well-formedness preservation (Theorema
54
), operational soundness (Theorem
56
) and completeness (Theorem
57
), and success sensitivity (Theorem
61
).

Definition 51

(Encoding on Contexts)
.

We define an encoding
\{\!\!\{\cdot\}\!\!\}
on contexts:

\displaystyle\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle=\Theta

\displaystyle\{\!\!\{\emptyset\}\!\!\}=\emptyset

\displaystyle\{\!\!\{x:\tau,\Gamma\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle=x:\tau,\{\!\!\{\Gamma\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle(x\not\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma))

\displaystyle\{\!\!\{x:\tau,\cdots,x:\tau,\Gamma\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle=x:\tau\wedge\cdots\wedge\tau,\{\!\!\{\Gamma\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle(x\not\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma))

Proposition 52
.

Let
M,N
be terms. We have:

1.

{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\{\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/x{|\!\}}
.

2.

{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle
, where
\widetilde{x}=x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}
is sequence of pairwise distinct fresh variables.

Proof.

By induction of the structure of
M
.
∎

Proposition 53

(Well-formedness Preservation under Linear Substitutions in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

Let
{M}\in\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
.
If
\Theta;\Gamma,x:\sigma\models{M}:\tau

and
\Theta;\Gamma\models x_{i}:\sigma
then

\Theta;\Gamma,x_{i}:\sigma\models{M}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{i}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle:\tau
.

Proof.

Standard, by induction on the well-formedness derivation rules in Fig.
3
.
∎

Proposition 54

(Well-formedness preservation for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}-{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
)
.

Let
B
and
\mathbb{M}
be a bag and a expression in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, respectively.

1.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\models B:(\sigma^{k},\eta)

and
\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)=\mathsf{mlfv}(B)

then
\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\};\{\!\!\{\Gamma\}\!\!\}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:(\sigma^{k},\eta)
and
\forall\ x:\pi\in\Gamma,\ \pi=\tau
for some
\tau
.

2.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\sigma

and
\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)=\mathsf{mlfv}(\mathbb{M})

then
\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\};\{\!\!\{\Gamma\}\!\!\}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
and
\forall\ x:\pi\in\Gamma,\ \pi=\tau
for some
\tau
.

Theorem 55

(Well-formedness Preservation for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}-{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
)
.

Let
B
and
\mathbb{M}
be a bag and an expression in
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, respectively.

1.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\models B:(\sigma^{k},\eta)

and
\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)=\mathsf{fv}(B)

then
\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\};\{\!\!\{\Gamma\}\!\!\}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}:(\sigma^{k},\eta)
.

2.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\sigma

and
\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)=\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{M})

then
\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\};\{\!\!\{\Gamma\}\!\!\}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}:\sigma
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on the typing derivations
\Theta;\Gamma\models B:(\sigma^{k},\eta)
and
\Theta;\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\sigma
, exploiting Proposition
54
. The analysis for bags Part 1. follows directly from the IHs and will be omitted.
As for Part 2. there are two main cases to consider:

1.

\mathbb{M}=M
.

Without loss of generality, assume
\mathsf{fv}(M)=\{x,y\}
. Then,

\Theta;\hat{x}:\sigma_{1}^{j},\hat{y}:\sigma_{2}^{k}\models M:\tau

where
\#(x,M)=j
and

\#(y,M)=k
, for some positive integers
j
and
k
.

After
j+k
applications of Proposition
53

we obtain:

\Theta;x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}\models M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle:\tau

where
\widetilde{x}=x_{1},\cdots,x_{j}

and
\widetilde{y}=y_{1},\cdots,y_{k}
.
From Proposition
54

one has

\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\};\{\!\!\{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}\}\!\!\}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

Since
\{\!\!\{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}\}\!\!\}=x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}
and
\{\!\!\{\Theta\}\!\!\}=\Theta
,
we have the following derivation:

\Theta;{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS:share]}

\Theta;x:\sigma_{1}^{j},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS:share]}

\Theta;x:\sigma_{1}^{j},y:\sigma_{2}^{k}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x][\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

By expanding Def.
24
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x][\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]

which completes the proof for this case.

2.

\mathbb{M}=M_{1}+\cdots+M_{n}
:

This case proceeds easily by IH, using Rule
\mathtt{[FS:sum]}
.

∎

Theorem 56

(Operational Completeness)
.

Let
\mathbb{M},\mathbb{N}
be well-formed
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
expressions.
Suppose
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\mathbb{M}
.

1.

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{\leq 2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

2.

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{+}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, for some
\mathbb{M}
.

3.

If
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
and
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the the rule from Fig.
1
applied to infer
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
, distinguishing the three cases: (below
\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}
abbreviates

[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\cdots[\widetilde{x_{n}}\leftarrow x_{n}]
).

1.

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
.

In this case,

\mathbb{N}=(\lambda x.M^{\prime})B
, where
B=C\star U
, the reduction is

\mathtt{[R:Beta]}

(\lambda x.M)B\longrightarrow M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

and
\mathbb{M}=M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Below we assume
\mathsf{lfv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\}
and
\widetilde{x_{i}}=x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{j_{i}}}
, where
j_{i}=\#(x_{i},N)
, for
1\leq i\leq k
.
On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}(\lambda x.M^{\prime})B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}((\lambda x.M^{\prime})B)\langle{{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x_{1}}}\rangle\cdots\langle{{\widetilde{x_{k}}}/{x_{k}}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

(1)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}(\lambda x.M^{{}^{\prime\prime}})B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}=({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\lambda x.M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=((\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\langle{{\widetilde{y}}/{x}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\langle{{\widetilde{y}}/{x}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}=\mathbb{L}

On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x_{1}}}\rangle\cdots\langle{{\widetilde{x_{k}}}/{x_{k}}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

(2)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

We need to analyze two sub-cases: either
\#({x},M)=\mathsf{size}(C)
or
\#({x},M)=k\geq 0
and our first sub-case is not met.

(a)

If
\#({x},M)=\mathsf{size}(C)
then we can reduce
\mathbb{L}
as: (via
\mathtt{[RS:Ex-sub]}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\widetilde{y}}$}\!/\mbox{\small${x}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(n)/{y_{n}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

From (
1
) and (
2
) and
{\widetilde{y}}={y_{1}}\ldots{y_{n}}
, one has the result.

(b)

Otherwise,
\#({x},M)=n\geq 0
.

Expanding the encoding in (
2
) :

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}=({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\langle{{\widetilde{y}}/{x}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

Therefore
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\mathbb{L}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
.

2.

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{\ell}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}

C={\lbag N_{1}\rbag}\cdot\dots\cdot{\lbag N_{k}\rbag}\ ,\ k\geq 1

\#({x},M)=k

\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{\ell}]}

M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N_{1}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{1})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+\cdots+M{\{\!|}N_{k}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{k})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

with
\mathbb{M}=M{\{\!|}N_{1}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{1})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+\cdots+M{\{\!|}N_{k}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{k})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\}
and
\widetilde{x_{i}}=x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{j_{i}}}
, where
j_{i}=\#(x_{i},N)
, for
1\leq i\leq k
.
On the one hand, we have: (last rule is
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x_{1}}}\rangle\cdots\langle{{\widetilde{x_{k}}}/{x_{k}}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C^{\prime}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle{\widetilde{y}}/{x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\{\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(2)/{y}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}

We assume for simplicity that
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime\prime})={y}_{1}

On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{1})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+\cdots+M{\{\!|}N_{k}/{x}{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\setminus N_{k})\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\{\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(2)/{y}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}

From these developments from
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, and
{\widetilde{y}}={y_{1}}\ldots{y_{n}}
, one has the result.

3.

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{!}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]\quad U_{i}=\lbag N\rbag^{!}

\mathtt{[R:Fetch^{!}]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

with
\mathbb{M}=M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.
Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\}
and
\widetilde{x_{i}}=x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{j_{i}}}
, where
j_{i}=\#(x_{i},N)
, for
1\leq i\leq k
.

On the one hand, we have: (the last rule is
\mathtt{[RS:Fetch^{!}]}
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x_{1}}}\rangle\cdots\langle{{\widetilde{x_{k}}}/{x_{k}}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

(3)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C^{\prime}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle{\widetilde{y}}/{x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(2)/{y}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}=\mathbb{L}

On the other hand, assuming for simplicity that
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime\prime})={x}[i]
and
U_{i}=N
, we have

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

(4)

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\{\!|}N/{x}[i]{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(2)/{y}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}=\mathbb{L}

From (
3
) and (
4
), one has the result.

4.

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
and
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
. There are two possible cases:

(a)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fail^{\ell}]}

Then
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\#({x},M)\neq\mathsf{size}(C)

\widetilde{z}=(\mathsf{mlfv}(M)\!\setminus x)\uplus\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\mathtt{[R:Fail^{\ell}]}

M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}

where
\mathbb{M}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
. Below assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x_{1}}\rangle\cdots\langle{\widetilde{x_{n}}}/{x_{n}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C^{\prime}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle y_{1},\cdots,{y_{k}}/{x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{y_{1}},\cdots,{y_{k}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C^{\prime}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS:Fail^{\ell}]}}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y},{\widetilde{x_{1}}},\cdots,{\widetilde{x_{n}}}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=\mathbb{L}

On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y},{\widetilde{x_{1}}},\cdots,{\widetilde{x_{n}}}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=\mathbb{L}

Therefore,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and the result follows.

(b)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fail^{!}]}

Then
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\#({x},M)=\mathsf{size}(C)

U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]

\mathtt{[R:Fail^{!}]}

M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

where
\mathbb{M}=M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\}
and
\widetilde{x_{i}}=x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{j_{i}}}
, where
j_{i}=\#(x_{i},N)
, for
1\leq i\leq k
.

On the one hand, we have: (the last rule applied was
\mathtt{[RS:Fail^{!}]}
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x_{1}}}\rangle\cdots\langle{{\widetilde{x_{k}}}/{x_{k}}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C^{\prime}\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle{\widetilde{y}}/{x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(2)/{y}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}

We assume for simplicity that
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime\prime})={x}[i]
.
On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(2)/{y}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}

From the
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
above one has the result.

(c)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{N}=(\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}})\ C\star U
and the reduction is

\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}

(\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}})\ C\star U\longrightarrow{}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}

and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}
. Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ C\star U\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}\ \ C^{\prime}\star U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}\star U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}\star U{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{1}]}}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=\mathbb{L}

Where
\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}=\widetilde{x_{1}},\cdots,\widetilde{x_{n}}
. On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\uplus\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=\mathbb{L}

Therefore,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, and the result follows.

(d)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}

Then
\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$z$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\#(z,\widetilde{y})=\mathsf{size}(C)\quad\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{mlfv}(C)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$z$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow{}\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{x}\setminus z)\uplus\widetilde{z}}

and
\mathbb{M}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}
.
Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$z$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$z$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

(5)

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}\langle{\widetilde{y}}/{x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\setminus{\widetilde{y}})\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{4}]}}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\setminus{\widetilde{y}})\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

As
\widetilde{y}
consists of free variables, we have that in
\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle
the substitutions also occur on
\widetilde{y}
resulting in a new
\widetilde{y^{\prime}}
where all
x_{i}
’s are replaced with their fresh components in
\widetilde{x_{i}}
. Similarly
\widetilde{y^{\prime\prime}}
is
\widetilde{y^{\prime}}
with each
x
replaced with a fresh
y_{i}
. On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\setminus{\widetilde{y}})\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

(6)

The reductions in (
5
) and (
6
) lead to identical expressions.

As before, the reduction via rule
\mathtt{[R]}
could occur inside a context (cf. Rules
\mathtt{[R:TCont]}
and
\mathtt{[R:ECont]}
). We consider only the case when the contextual rule used is
\mathtt{[R:TCont]}
. We have
\mathbb{N}=C[N]
. When we have
C[N]\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}C[M]
such that
N\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}M
we need to show that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C[N]{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{j}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C[M]{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
for some
j
dependent on
{\mathtt{[R]}}
. Firstly let us assume
{\mathtt{[R]}}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}
then we take
j=1
. Let us take
C[\cdot]
to be
[\cdot]B
and
\mathsf{fv}(NB)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
then

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

We take
N^{\prime}B^{\prime}=NB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle
, we have by the IH that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
and hence we can deduce that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
where
M^{\prime}B^{\prime}=MB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle
. Finally we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

and hence
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C[N]{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C[M]{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
.
∎

Theorem 57

(Operational Soundness)
.

Let
\mathbb{N}
be a well-formed
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
expression.
Suppose
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}
. Then, there exists
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and

1.

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow^{\leq 1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

2.

If
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow^{*}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, for
\mathbb{N}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}\equiv_{\lambda}\mathbb{N}^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{N}
:

1.

Cases
\mathbb{N}={x}
,
\mathbb{N}=x[i]
,
\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
and
\mathbb{N}=\lambda x.N
, are trivial, since no reductions can be performed.

2.

\mathbb{N}=NB
:

Suppose
\mathsf{lfv}(NB)=\{{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{n}\}
. Then,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB\langle{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x}_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle{\widetilde{x_{n}}}/{x}_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

(7)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

where
{\widetilde{x_{i}}}={x}_{i1},\ldots,{x}_{ij_{i}}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
.
By the reduction rules in Fig.
13
there are three possible reductions starting in
\mathbb{N}
:

(a)

{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}
reduces via a
\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}
.

In this case
N=\lambda x.N_{1}
, and the encoding in (
7
) gives
N^{\prime}=N\langle{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x}_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle{\widetilde{x_{n}}}/{x}_{n}\rangle
, which implies
N^{\prime}=\lambda x.N_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}
and the following holds:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}(\lambda x.N^{\prime}_{1}){\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}

\displaystyle=(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}_{1}\langle{\widetilde{y}}/{x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{[\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}])=(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}])

Thus, we have the following
\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}
reduction from (
7
):

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

(8)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}=\mathbb{L}

Notice that the expression
\mathbb{N}
can perform the following
\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
reduction:

\mathbb{N}=(\lambda x.N_{1})B\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R:Beta]}}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

Assuming
\mathbb{N^{\prime}}=N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and we take

, there are two cases:

\#({x},M)=\mathsf{size}(C)=k
.

On the one hand,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{\widetilde{x_{1}}}/{x}_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle{\widetilde{x_{n}}}/{x}_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}\langle{y}_{1},\cdots,{y}_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/{y}_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

On the other hand, via application of rule
\mathtt{[RS:Ex\text{-}Sub]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

and the result follows.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{n}]}

There are five possible reductions that can take place, these being
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}

iii.

Suppose that
N^{\prime\prime}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}
proceed similarly then from (
10
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(n)/{y}_{n}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

where

. We also have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime},\text{ where }\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{mfv}(B).

and so the result follows.

Suppose that
N^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow N^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
This case follows by the induction hypothesis

(b)

Otherwise, we continue from equation (
9
), where

, as follows

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[{y}_{1}.\cdots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]}

We also have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}

Then,

4.

\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}_{1}+\mathbb{N}_{2}

∎

E.3
Success Sensitiveness of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

We now consider success sensitiveness, a property that complements (and relies on) operational completeness and soundness. For the purposes of the proof, we consider the extension of
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
with dedicated constructs and predicates that specify success.

Definition 58
.

We extend the syntax of terms for
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
with the same
\checkmark
construct.
In both cases, we assume
\checkmark
is well formed.
Also, we also define
\mathsf{head}(\checkmark)=\checkmark
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\checkmark{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=\checkmark

An expression
\mathbb{M}
has success, denoted

\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
, when there is a sequence of reductions from
\mathbb{M}
that leads to an expression that includes a summand that contains an occurrence of
\checkmark
in head position.

Definition 59

(Success in
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
)
.

In
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, we define

\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
if and only if
there exist
M_{1},\cdots,M_{k}
such that

\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow^{*}M_{1}+\cdots+M_{k}
and

\mathsf{head}(M_{j}^{\prime})=\checkmark
, for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
and term
M_{j}^{\prime}
such that
M_{j}\equiv_{\lambda}M_{j}^{\prime}
.

Notation 7
.

We use the notation
\mathsf{head}_{\sum}(\mathbb{M})
to be that
\forall M_{i},M_{j}\in\mathbb{M}
we have that
\mathsf{head}(M_{i})=\mathsf{head}(M_{j})
hence we say that
\mathsf{head}_{\sum}(\mathbb{M})=\mathsf{head}(M_{i})
for some
M_{i}\in\mathbb{M}

Proposition 60

(Preservation of Head term)
.

The head of a term is preserved when applying the encoding
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\text{-}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
. That is to say:

\forall M\in\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}\quad\mathsf{head}(M)=\checkmark\iff\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})=\checkmark

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
M
. We only need to consider terms of the following form.

1.

When
M=\checkmark
the case is immediate.

2.

When
M=NB
with
\mathsf{fv}(NB)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
and
\#(x_{i},M)=j_{i}
we have that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\cdots[\widetilde{x_{k}}\leftarrow x_{k}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})

and
\mathsf{head}(NB)=\mathsf{head}(N)
, by the IH we have
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark\iff\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})=\checkmark
.

3.

When
M=N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, we must have that
\#(x,M)=\mathsf{size}(C)
for the head of this term to be
\checkmark
. Let
\mathsf{fv}(N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
and
\#(x_{i},M)=j_{i}
. We have that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1}\leftarrow x_{k}]})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}(\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N\langle\widetilde{x}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N\langle\widetilde{x}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N\langle\widetilde{x}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})

and
\mathsf{head}(N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}(N)
, by the IH
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark\iff\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})=\checkmark
.

∎

Theorem 61

(Success Sensitivity)
.

Let
\mathbb{M}
be a well-formed expression.
We have

\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
if and only if
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of expressions
\mathit{u}\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
.

1.

Suppose that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By operational completeness (Theorem
56
) we have that if
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\mathbb{M^{\prime}}
then

(a)

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{\leq 2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

(b)

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{+}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, for some
\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\equiv_{\lambda}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
.

(c)

If
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
and
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

Notice that neither our reduction rules (in Def.
13
), or our congruence
\equiv_{\lambda}
(in Fig.
23
), or our encoding (
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\checkmark{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\checkmark
) create or destroy a
\checkmark
occurring in the head of term. By Proposition
60
the encoding preserves the head of a term being
\checkmark
. The encoding acts homomorphically over sums, therefore, if a
\checkmark
appears as the head of a term in a sum, it will stay in the encoded sum. We can iterate the operational completeness lemma and obtain the result.

2.

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By operational soundness (Theorem
57
) we have that if
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}
then there exist
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and

(a)

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow^{\leq 1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

(b)

If
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow^{*}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, for
\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\equiv_{\lambda}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
.

Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{*}M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
, and
\mathsf{head}(M_{j}^{\prime})=\checkmark
, for some
j
and
M_{j}^{\prime}
, s.t.
M_{j}\equiv_{\lambda}M_{j}^{\prime}
.

Notice that if
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
is itself a term headed with
\checkmark
, say
\mathsf{head}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})=\checkmark
, then
\mathbb{M}
is itself headed with
\checkmark
, from Proposition
60
.
In the case
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
,
k\geq 2
, and
\checkmark
occurs in the head of an
M_{j}
, the reasoning is similar.
\mathbb{M}
has one of the forms:

(a)

\mathbb{M}=N_{1}
, then
N_{1}
must contain the subterm
M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and
\mathsf{size}(C)=\#(x,M)
.

The encoding of
\mathbb{M}
is

{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}C{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/x_{i}{|\!\rangle}\ldots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(k)/x_{i}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
.
We can apply Proposition
60
and the result follows.

(b)

\mathbb{M}=N_{1}+\ldots+N_{l}
for
l\geq 2
.

This reasoning is similar and uses the fact that the encoding distributes homomorphically over sums.

In the case where
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{+}M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
, and
\mathsf{head}(M_{j}^{\prime})=\checkmark
, for some
j
and
M_{j}^{\prime}
, such that
M_{j}\equiv_{\lambda}M_{j}^{\prime}
, the reasoning is similar to the previous, since our reduction rules do not introduce/eliminate
\checkmark
occurring in the head of terms.

∎

Appendix F
Appendix to Subsection

F.1
Type Preservation

Lemma 62
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{1},m)}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{2},n)}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}(\sigma^{j},\eta){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{1},m)}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}(\sigma^{k},\eta){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{2},n)}
hold, provided that
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},n
and
m

1.

If
j>k
then take
\tau_{1}
to be an arbitrary type,
m=0
, take
\tau_{2}
to be
\sigma
and
n=j-k
.

2.

If
j<k
then take
\tau_{1}
to be
\sigma
,
m=k-j
, take
\tau_{2}
to be an arbitrary type and
n=0
.

3.

Otherwise, if
j=k
then take
m=n=0
. In this case,
\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}
are unimportant.

Proof.

We shall prove the case of
(1)
for the first equality, and the case for the second equality and of
(2)
are analogous. The case of
(3)
follows by the encoding on types in Def.
28
.

Hence take
j,k,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},m,n
satisfying the conditions in (1):
j>k
,
\tau_{1}
to be an arbitrary type,
m=0
,
\tau_{2}=\sigma
and
n=j-k
.
We want to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{1},0)}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\sigma,n)}
. In fact,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\sigma,n)}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{k-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\sigma,n)}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\sigma,n)}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{1},0)}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sigma^{j-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{(\tau_{1},0)}

Notice that
