8.

Case
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{1}]}
:

Then,

\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U
and
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}=\mathbb{M}
where
\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(C)
.
Notice that

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)(v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

and the result follows.

9.

Cases
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{2}]}
,
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{3}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{4}]}
:

These cases follow by IH similarly to the previous.

∎

F.2.2
Soundness

Theorem 67

(Well Formed Weak Operational Soundness)
.

Let
\mathbb{N}
be a
well-formed, linearly closed
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
expression.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}Q

then there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that

Q\longrightarrow^{*}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{*}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\equiv Q^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{N}
and then induction on the number of reductions of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}\longrightarrow^{*}Q
.

1.

Base case:

\mathbb{N}={x}
,
\mathbb{N}={x}[j]
,
\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}
and
\mathbb{N}=\lambda x.(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])
.
.

No reductions can take place, and the result follows trivially.

Q={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
and
{x}\longrightarrow^{0}{x}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

2.

\mathbb{N}=M(C\star U)
.

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))
, and we are able to perform the reductions from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
.

We now proceed by induction on
k
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q
. There are two main cases:

(a)

When
k=0
the thesis follows easily:

We have

Q={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
and
M(C\star U)\longrightarrow^{0}M(C\star U)=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

(b)

The interesting case is when
k\geq 1
.

Then, for some process
R
and
n,m
such that
k=n+m
, we have the following:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{m}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))\longrightarrow^{n}Q

Thus, the first
m\geq 0
reduction steps are internal to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}
; type preservation in
\mathsf{s}\pi
ensures that, if they occur, these reductions do not discard the possibility of synchronizing with
v.\mathtt{some}
. Then, the first of the
n\geq 0
reduction steps towards
Q
is a synchronization between
R
and
v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)}
.

We consider two sub-cases, depending on the values of
m
and
n
:

i.

m=0
and
n\geq 1
:

Then
R={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\longrightarrow^{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}
.
Notice that there are two possibilities of having an unguarded:

A.

M=(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil\quad(p,q\geq 0)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}

\displaystyle=(\nu y_{1},\cdots,y_{p},z^{!}_{1},\cdots,z^{!}_{q})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N_{1})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{y_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{p}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N_{p})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N_{p}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{y_{p}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!z^{!}_{1}.(z_{1}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{z_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!z^{!}_{q}.(z_{q}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{z_{q}})

\displaystyle=(\nu\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=(\nu\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z})(v.\overline{\mathtt{some}};v(x).x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q^{\prime\prime})

where
\widetilde{y}=y_{1},\cdots,y_{p}
.
\widetilde{z}=z^{!}_{1},\cdots,z^{!}_{q}
and

\displaystyle Q^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N_{1})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{y_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{p}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N_{p})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N_{p}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{y_{p}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!z^{!}_{1}.(z_{1}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{z_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!z^{!}_{q}.(z_{q}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{z_{q}}.

With this shape for
M
, we then have the following:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}(M\ B){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v,\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z})(v(x).x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q^{\prime\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v,\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z},x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q^{\prime\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}[v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})

\displaystyle=Q_{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z},x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

We also have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle=(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil(C\star U)

\displaystyle\equiv_{\lambda}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])(C\star U)){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\star U)$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil=\mathbb{M}

Furthermore, we have:

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\displaystyle\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\star U)$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z},x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}Q^{\prime\prime})

We consider different possibilities for
n\geq 1
; in all the cases, the result follows.

When
n=1
:

We have
Q=Q_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{1}Q_{1}
.
We also have that

•

Q_{1}\longrightarrow^{2}Q_{3}=Q^{\prime}
,

•

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}

•

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{3}
.

When
n=2
:
the analysis is similar.

When
n\geq 3
:

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{3}Q_{3}\longrightarrow^{l}Q
, for
l\geq 0
. We also know that
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
,
Q_{3}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
. By the IH, there exist
Q^{\prime},\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Finally,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{3}Q_{3}\longrightarrow^{l}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

B.

M=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}
.

Then,
\begin{aligned} {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}&={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}=v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}.\end{aligned}

With this shape for
M
, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}(M\ (C\star U)){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)(v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

We also have that

\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\mathsf{lfv}(C)}=\mathbb{M}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\uplus\mathsf{lfv}(C)}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\uplus\mathsf{lfv}(C)}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(C)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\mathsf{lfv}(C).\overline{\mathtt{none}}.

ii.

When
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
, we distinguish two cases:

A.

When
n=0
:

Then,
\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))=Q
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{m}R
where
m\geq 1
. Then by the IH there exist
R^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
such that
R\longrightarrow^{i}R^{\prime}
,
M\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=R^{\prime}
. Hence we have that

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{m}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))=Q

We also know that

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{i}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)(R^{\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))=Q^{\prime}

and so the
u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
term can reduce as follows:
\mathbb{N}=(M\ (C\star U))\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}M^{\prime}\ (C\star U)=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

B.

When
n\geq 1
:

Then
R
has an occurrence of an unguarded
v.\overline{\mathtt{some}}
or
v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
, hence it is of the form

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/{z_{1}}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/{z_{q}}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}.

This case follows by IH.

This concludes the analysis for the case
\mathbb{N}=(M\,(C\star U))
.

3.

\mathbb{N}=M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]
.

The sharing variable
{x}
is not free and the result follows by vacuity.

4.

\mathbb{N}=M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})

Let us consider three cases.

(a)

When
\mathsf{size}({\widetilde{x}})=\mathsf{size}(C)
.
Then let us consider the shape of the bag
C
.

i.

When
C=\mathtt{1}
.

We have the following

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})

\displaystyle=(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{x}(x^{\ell}).

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\overline{x}(x^{!}).(!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x.\overline{\mathtt{close}})))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x)(x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\overline{x}(x^{\ell}).({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\overline{x}(x^{!}).

\displaystyle(!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x.\overline{\mathtt{close}})))

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x,x^{\ell})(x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\overline{x}(x^{!}).

\displaystyle(!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x.\overline{\mathtt{close}}))

\displaystyle=Q_{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x,x^{\ell},x^{!})(x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x.\overline{\mathtt{close}})

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{4}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})(x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{i}).(y_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{n}).(y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})(\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{i}).(y_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}(y_{n}).(y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{5}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!},y_{i})(y_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{i}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{i}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{6}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!},y_{i})(y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{i}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{7}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{8}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=Q_{9}

Notice how
Q_{8}
has a choice however the
x^{\ell}
name can be closed at any time so for simplicity we only perform communication across this name once all other names have completed their reductions.

Now we proceed by induction on the number of reductions
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q
.

A.

When
k=0
, the result follows trivially. Just take
\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q=Q^{\prime}
.

B.

When
k=1
.

We have
Q=Q_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{1}Q_{1}

We also have that
Q_{1}\longrightarrow^{8}Q_{9}=Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil=M
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{9}

C.

When
2\leq k\leq 8
.

Proceeds similarly to the previous case

D.

When
k\geq 9
.

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{9}Q_{9}\longrightarrow^{l}Q
, for
l\geq 0
. Since
Q_{9}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
we apply the induction hypothesis we have that there exist
Q^{\prime},\mathbb{N}^{\prime}\ s.t.\ Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime},M\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{5}Q_{5}\longrightarrow^{l}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and by the contextual reduction rule it follows that
\mathbb{N}=(M[\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$1$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and the case holds.

ii.

When
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdot\lbag N_{l}\rbag
, for
l\geq 1
.
Then,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{4}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})(x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{l})};x^{\ell}({x}_{1}).\cdots x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{l}).(y_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{l}.\mathtt{close};0

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus{x}_{l})};x^{\ell}({x}_{l}).x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};

\displaystyle y_{l+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\cdots)\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C)};x^{\ell}(y_{1}).x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{lfv}(C)};

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}({x}_{1}).({x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};

\displaystyle x^{\ell}(y_{l}).x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{l},\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}({x}_{l}).({x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})))

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{5l}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!},{x}_{1},y_{1},\cdots,{x}_{l},y_{1})(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots y_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};

\displaystyle y_{l}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{l+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots{x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{5}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{!},{x}_{1},y_{1},\cdots,{x}_{l},y_{1})(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots y_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{l}.\mathtt{close};0

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots{x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}}

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{l}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{!},{x}_{1}\cdots,{x}_{l})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{6l+9}

The proof follows by induction on the number of reductions
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q
.

A.

When
k=0
, the result follows trivially. Just take
\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q=Q^{\prime}
.

B.

When
1\leq k\leq 6l+9
.

Let
Q_{k}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q_{k}
.
We also have that
Q_{k}\longrightarrow^{6l+9-k}Q_{6l+9}=Q^{\prime}
,

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{6l+9}
.

C.

When
k>6l+9
.

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{6l+9}Q_{6l+9}\longrightarrow^{n}Q
for
n\geq 1
. Also,

\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\text{ and }\\
&Q_{6l+9}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}.\end{aligned}

By the induction hypothesis, there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that

\ Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
,

\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

Finally,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{6l+9}Q_{6l+9}\longrightarrow^{n}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and

\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}M{\langle\!|}C_{i}(1)/{x_{1}}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{i}(l)/{x_{l}}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}.

(b)

When
\mathsf{size}(\widetilde{x})>\mathsf{size}(C)
.

Then we have

\mathbb{N}=M[{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
with
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdots\lbag N_{l}\rbag\quad k>l
.
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}=\mathbb{M}
and
\widetilde{z}=(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus\{{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)
. On the one hand, we have:
Hence
k=l+m
for some
m\geq 1

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x(x^{\ell}).x(x^{!}).x.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{4}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M[{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x^{\ell}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!})(x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k})};

\displaystyle x^{\ell}({x}_{1}).\cdots x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{k}).(y_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{k}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus{x}_{k})};x^{\ell}({x}_{k}).

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{k+1}).(y_{k+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{k+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\cdots)\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C)};x^{\ell}(y_{1}).x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{lfv}(C)};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}({x}_{1}).({x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};x^{\ell}(y_{l}).x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{y_{l},\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}({x}_{l}).({x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle\qquad P_{\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{l+5}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{\ell},x^{!},{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{l})(x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus{x}_{l+1},\cdots,{x}_{k})};x^{\ell}({x}_{l+1}).\cdots

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{k}).(y_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{k}.\mathtt{close};0\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus{x}_{k})};x^{\ell}({x}_{k}).

\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x^{\ell}}(y_{k+1}).(y_{k+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{k+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x^{\ell}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu x^{!},{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{l})(u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus\{{x}_{1},\cdots,{x}_{k}\}).\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle x}_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{1}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\cdots\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}_{l}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{l}\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}(\mathsf{lfv}(M)\setminus\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}).\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\mathsf{lfv}(C).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{7l+10}

The rest of the proof is by induction on the number of reductions
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{j}Q
.

i.

When
j=0
, the result follows trivially. Just take
\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q=Q^{\prime}
.

ii.

When
1\leq j\leq 7l+10
.

Let
Q_{j}
be such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{j}Q_{j}
.
By the steps above one has

\begin{aligned} &Q_{j}\longrightarrow^{7l+10-j}Q_{7l+6}=Q^{\prime},\\
&\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime};\text{ and}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{7l+10}.\end{aligned}

iii.

When
j>7l+10
.

In this case, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{7l+10}Q_{7l+10}\longrightarrow^{n}Q,

for
n\geq 1
.
We also know that

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}
. However no further reductions can be performed.

(c)

When
\mathsf{size}(\widetilde{x})<\mathsf{size}(C)
, the proof proceeds similarly to the previous case.

5.

\mathbb{N}=M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\rangle}
.

In this case,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})\longrightarrow^{m}(\nu{x})(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})\longrightarrow^{n}Q,

for some process
R
. Where
\longrightarrow^{n}
is a reduction that initially synchronizes with
{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})}
when
n\geq 1
,
n+m=k\geq 1
. Type preservation in
\mathsf{s}\pi
ensures reducing
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\longrightarrow^{m}
does not consume possible synchronizations with
{x}.\mathtt{some}
, if they occur. Let us consider the the possible sizes of both
m
and
n
.

(a)

For
m=0
and
n\geq 1
.

We have that
R={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
.

Notice that there are two possibilities of having an unguarded
x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}
or
x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
without internal reductions:

i.

M=\mathtt{fail}^{{x},\widetilde{y}}
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{{x},\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})(u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime}).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

Notice that no further reductions can be performed.
Thus,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\mathsf{fv}(N^{\prime}).\overline{\mathtt{none}}=Q^{\prime}.

We also have that
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

ii.

\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
.

By the diamond property we will be reducing each non-deterministic choice of a process simultaneously.
Then we have the following

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{x}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({x}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};[{x}\leftrightarrow j]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu{x})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})([{x}\leftrightarrow j]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})

\displaystyle=Q_{2}

In addition,

\mathbb{N}=M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\}}=\mathbb{M}

Finally,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\{\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})

\displaystyle=Q_{2}.

A.

When
n=1
:

Then,
Q=Q_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{1}Q_{1}

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\{\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{2}

B.

When
n\geq 2
:

Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{2}Q_{2}\longrightarrow^{l}Q
, for

. Also,

\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}
,
Q_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
. By the induction hypothesis, there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Finally,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{2}Q_{2}\longrightarrow^{l}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

(b)

For
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
.

i.

When
n=0
.

Then
(\nu{x})(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})=Q
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{m}R
where
m\geq 1
. By the IH there exist
R^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
such that
R\longrightarrow^{i}R^{\prime}
,
M\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=R^{\prime}
. Thus,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})\longrightarrow^{m}(\nu{x})(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})=Q

Also,

Q\longrightarrow^{i}(\nu{x})(R^{\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})=Q^{\prime},

and the term can reduce as follows:
\mathbb{N}=M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\sum_{M_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{M}^{\prime}}M_{i}^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/{x}{|\!\rangle}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}

ii.

When
n\geq 1
.
Then
R
has an occurrence of an unguarded
x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}
or
x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
, this case follows by IH.

6.

\mathbb{N}=M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

In this case,

\begin{aligned} {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}&=(\nu x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}).\end{aligned}

Then,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})\longrightarrow^{m}(\nu x^{!})(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})\longrightarrow^{n}Q.

for some process
R
. Where
\longrightarrow^{n}
is a reduction initially synchronises with
!x^{!}.(x_{i})
when
n\geq 1
,
n+m=k\geq 1
. Type preservation in
\mathsf{s}\pi
ensures reducing
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\longrightarrow^{m}
doesn’t consume possible synchronisations with
!x^{!}.(x_{i})
if they occur. Let us consider the the possible sizes of both

and

.

(a)

For

and

.

In this case,
R={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
as

Notice that the only possibility of having an unguarded
\overline{x^{!}}?({x_{i}})

By the diamond property we will be reducing each non-deterministic choice of a process simultaneously.
Then we have the following:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}{x}[ind]{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})(\overline{x^{!}}?({x_{i}}).{x}_{i}.l_{ind};[{x_{i}}\leftrightarrow j]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})((\nu x_{i})({x}_{i}.l_{ind};[{x_{i}}\leftrightarrow j]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}x_{i}.case(ind.{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U_{ind}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}}))\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})

\displaystyle\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})((\nu x_{i})([{x_{i}}\leftrightarrow j]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U_{ind}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U_{ind}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

We consider the two cases of the form of
U_{ind}
and show that the choice of

is inconsequential

•

When
U_{i}=\lbag N\rbag^{!}
:

In this case,

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{N}&=M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/x^{!}{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil=\mathbb{M}.\end{aligned}

and

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\{\!|}N/x^{!}{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\lbag N\rbag{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

•

When
U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}
:

In this case,

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{N}&=M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/x^{!}{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil=\mathbb{M}.\end{aligned}

Notice that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}^{!}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}=j.\mathtt{none}
and that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}=j.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
. In addition,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\{\!|}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/x^{!}{|\!\}}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})(\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}^{!}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{j}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}P_{i})\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

Both choices give an
\mathbb{M}
that are equivalent to
Q_{3}
.

i.

When
n\leq 2
.

In this case,
Q=Q_{n}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{n}Q_{n}
.

Also,
Q_{n}\longrightarrow^{3-n}Q_{3}=Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q_{2}
.

ii.

When
n\geq 3
.

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{3}Q_{3}\longrightarrow^{l}Q
for
l\geq 0
. We also know that
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}
,
Q_{3}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
. By the IH, there exist
Q
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Finally,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{2}Q_{3}\longrightarrow^{l}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

(b)

For
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
.

i.

When
n=0
.

Then
(\nu x^{!})(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})=Q
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{m}R
where
m\geq 1
. By the IH there exist
R^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
such that
R\longrightarrow^{i}R^{\prime}
,
M\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=R^{\prime}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})\longrightarrow^{m}(\nu x^{!})(R\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})=Q.

In addition,

Q\longrightarrow^{i}(\nu x^{!})(R^{\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})=Q
, and the term can reduce as follows:
\mathbb{N}=M\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\sum_{M_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{M}^{\prime}}M_{i}^{\prime}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

ii.

When
n\geq 1
.

Then
R
has an occurrence of an unguarded
\overline{x^{!}}?({x_{i}})
, and the case follows by IH.

∎

F.3
Success Sensitiveness of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}

We say that a process occurs
guarded
when it occurs behind a prefix (input, output, closing of channels, servers, server request, choice an selection and non-deterministic session behaviour). Formally,

Definition 68
.

A process
P\in\mathsf{s}\pi
is guarded if
\alpha.P
,
\alpha;P
or
{x}.\mathtt{case}_{i\in I}\{\mathtt{l}_{i}:P\}
, where
\alpha=\overline{x}(y),x(y),x.\overline{\mathtt{close}},x.\mathtt{close},

x.\overline{\mathtt{some}},x.\mathtt{some}_{(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})},{x}.\mathtt{l}_{i},!x(y),\overline{x}?(y)
.
We say it occurs
unguarded
if it is not guarded for any prefix.

Proposition 69

(Preservation of Success)
.

For all
M\in u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, the following hold:

1.

\mathsf{head}(M)=\checkmark\implies{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}=P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\checkmark\oplus Q

2.

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\checkmark\oplus Q\implies\mathsf{head}(M)=\checkmark

Proof.

Proof of both cases by induction on the structure of
M
.

1.

We only need to consider terms of the following form:

(a)

M=\checkmark
:

This case is immediate.

(b)

M=N\ (C\star U)
:

Then,
\mathsf{head}(N\ (C\star U))=\mathsf{head}(N)
. If
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x})).

By the IH,
\checkmark
is unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
.

(c)

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}

Then we have that
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=\checkmark
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})
and by the IH
\checkmark
is unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
.

(d)

M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil

Then we have that
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=\checkmark
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=(\nu x^{!})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}!x^{!}.(x_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x_{i}})
and by the IH
\checkmark
is unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
.

2.

We only need to consider terms of the following form:

(a)

Case
M=\checkmark
:

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\checkmark{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=\checkmark

which is an unguarded occurrence of
\checkmark
and that
\mathsf{head}(\checkmark)=\checkmark
.

(b)

Case
M=N(C\star U)
:

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=\bigoplus_{C_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(C)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}C_{i}\star U{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{x}))
. The only occurrence of an unguarded
\checkmark
can occur is within
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{v}
. By the IH,
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark
and finally
\mathsf{head}(N\ B)=\mathsf{head}(N)
.

(c)

Case
M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}
:

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=(\nu{x})({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}{x}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{lfv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{{x}})
, an unguarded occurrence of
\checkmark
can only occur within
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
. By the IH,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=\checkmark
and hence
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/{x}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})
.

(d)

Case
M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{x}\rrceil
:

This case is analogous to the previous.

∎

Theorem 70

(Success Sensitivity)
.

The encoding
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}:u\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}\rightarrow\mathsf{s}\pi
is success sensitive on well formed linearly closed expression if
for any expression we have
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

Proof.

We proceed with the proof in two parts.

1.

Suppose that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By Def.
59
, there exists
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M_{1},\cdots,M_{k}
such that
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow^{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{head}(M_{j}^{\prime})=\checkmark
, for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
and term
M_{j}^{\prime}
such that
M_{j}\equiv_{\lambda}M_{j}^{\prime}
.
By completeness, there exists
Q
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}Q={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
.

We wish to show that there exists
Q^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{*}Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}
has an unguarded occurrence of
\checkmark
.

From
Q={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
and due to compositionality and the homomorphic preservation of non-determinism we have that

\begin{aligned} Q&={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\\
\end{aligned}
.

By Proposition
69
(1) we have that
\mathsf{head}(M_{j})=\checkmark\implies{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}M_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\checkmark\oplus Q^{\prime}
. Hence
Q
reduces to a process that has an unguarded occurence of
\checkmark
.

2.

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By operational soundness (Lemma
67
) we have that if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}Q

then there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that

Q\longrightarrow^{*}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{*}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}P_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus P_{k}
, and
P_{j}^{\prime}=P_{j}^{\prime\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\checkmark
, for some
j
and
P_{j}^{\prime}
, such that
P_{j}\equiv P_{j}^{\prime}
.

Notice that if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}
is itself a term with unguarded
\checkmark
, say
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=P\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\checkmark
, then
\mathbb{M}
is itself headed with
\checkmark
, from Proposition
69
(2).

In the case
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rrbracket}_{u}=P_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus P_{k}
,
k\geq 2
, and
\checkmark
occurs unguarded in an
P_{j}
, The encoding acts homomorphically over sums and the reasoning is similar. We have that
P_{j}=P_{j}^{\prime}\mathord{\;\mathbf{|}\;}\checkmark
we apply Proposition
69
(2).

∎
